# S00 Platform Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Move finflow from the broken Cloudflare Pages / OpenNext build to Next.js 16 on Vercel (functions in `fra1`, Node 24) with a trusted `firebase-admin` server layer, member-scoped Firestore rules, a hardened Enable Banking flow without the test backdoor, and a CI test contour (lint, typecheck, Vitest, rules tests, Playwright smoke on emulators, build) that protects `master`, without adding product features.

**Architecture:** All code work happens on one branch, `s00-platform`, in five parts: A (repo cleanup, Cloudflare removal, Next 16), B (Vitest harness, `lib/server/*`, `/api/health`), C (signed Enable Banking `state`, Bearer-token and membership guards, backdoor removal), D (`firebase.json`, rules, indexes, rules tests, emulator wiring) and E (Playwright, CI, Vercel project and env, PR and merge, production checks, branch protection, Cloudflare shutdown). The branch ships through a single PR. Once the production deployment is green, the rules go to `finflow-e94b5` (D6), then the Enable Banking redirect, branch protection and the Cloudflare decommission follow. Secrets move only from the gitignored `.env`, `.env.local` and `.secrets/` to Vercel through `scripts/vercel.mjs`, and are never printed or committed.

**Tech Stack:** Next.js 16.4.2 (Turbopack, `proxy.ts`), React 19.2.9, TypeScript 5, Tailwind 4 / shadcn, Firebase JS SDK 12, firebase-admin 14.5, Vitest 5, @firebase/rules-unit-testing 5.0.2, Firebase CLI 15 emulators (Temurin 21), Playwright, GitHub Actions, Vercel REST API, ESLint 9 + eslint-config-next 16.4.2, npm on Node 24, Git Bash on Windows.

---

**How to read this plan**

- Tasks are numbered 1–29 in **execution order**. Each heading keeps the original part id in parentheses, for example `### Task 25: ... (E5)`. Cross-references inside the tasks use those part ids ("Task E4 Step 8", "Part D, Task D6"). The table in **Execution order** maps them to the global numbers.
- Two tasks sit outside their part's block because of when they must run: **Task 24 (A4)** documents every other part and has to be the last commit before Task 25 marks the S00 PR ready for merge, and **Task 26 (D6)** deploys the rules only after production runs the matching code.
- Every part keeps its own conventions block (Git Bash, cwd `F:/AI/finflow`, CRLF handling, never printing secrets). They apply to every task of that part, wherever the task sits.
- **Working directory.** The session's primary working directory is `F:/AI/REVERSE ENGINEER`. That is a *different* git repo, and it also has a `package.json`, `tests/fixtures/`, `vitest.config.ts`, `scripts/` and `.github/`. The Bash cwd resets between tool calls. So every bash block in this plan starts with `cd /f/AI/finflow || exit 1`, and every block that writes, deletes, installs, commits or pushes also checks the repo first with `[ "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" = "F:/AI/finflow" ] || { echo WRONG REPO; exit 1; }`. Keep both lines when you copy a block, and never run a block without them. File paths in this plan are relative to `F:/AI/finflow`. With the Write, Edit and Read tools always pass the absolute path, for example `F:/AI/finflow/lib/server/http-error.ts`, never `lib/server/http-error.ts`.
- Secrets never appear in this plan. Commands read them from `F:/AI/finflow/.env`, `F:/AI/finflow/.env.local` and `F:/AI/finflow/.secrets/` and print only names, lengths or PASS/FAIL. The repo `remit-agency/finflow` is **public**, so this plan also avoids personal operational details: the Firebase CLI account comes from `FIREBASE_ACCOUNT` in `.env` (Task 15 Step 2), and the web app id from `.env.local`.

**Scope decisions (confirm the first one with the user before execution)**

- **`/pricing` cleanup (Task 1 Step 5), needs the user's OK.** The spec names only the landing page ("обіцянки неіснуючих функцій на лендінгу"). This plan also removes the same false claims (team members, auto-categorisation, CSV import, mobile app) from `/pricing`, because the landing page links to it. Present this to the user together with the plan and get an explicit OK. If the user declines: skip Task 1 Step 5; in Task 1 Steps 2 and 6 run the `grep` on `app/page.tsx` only (expected `11` before, `0` after) and lint only `app/page.tsx`; leave `app/pricing/page.tsx` out of Task 1 Step 9 (`git add`) and drop its `M` line from the expected status there (Step 10 then reports `37 files changed`); in Task 2 Step 9 delete the `pricing has no team / auto-categorisation claims` check line from the smoke script (every later smoke run then prints 12 `PASS` lines instead of 13); and drop the `/pricing` wording from the Task 1 Step 10 commit message and the Task 24 Step 6 `PROGRESS.md` entry.
- The Enable Banking `accounts` route keeps its current upstream endpoint (`GET /sessions/{id}/accounts`) in S00 and only gains the auth, membership and validation guards (Task 11). The Enable Banking API reference has no such endpoint (it has `GET /sessions/{id}`), but no UI calls this route. The endpoint fix is recorded as known debt for S09 (Task 24 Step 6), because the spec says S00 adds no product behaviour.

This order differs from the part order in one place: Task 24 (A4) moves from the end of S00 to just before Task 25 (E5). A4 commits to `s00-platform` and its Step 1 needs files from Parts B–E (E3's `ci.yml`, E4's `vercel.json`). After E5 merges and deletes that branch, and once E7 protects `master`, a later A4 commit would have nowhere to go.

**Parallel work.** The default is strictly sequential execution in one working tree: every task commits to `s00-platform`, and two agents in the same tree would mix their staged changes. Only these overlaps are safe:

- After Task 8, chain C (Tasks 9–14) and chain D1–D5 (Tasks 15–19) touch disjoint files. Only Task 17 changes `package.json` / `package-lock.json`. They may run in parallel in **separate git worktrees** (superpowers:using-git-worktrees) branched from Task 8's commit. A new worktree has neither the gitignored `.env.local` nor `node_modules`, so in each worktree first run `cp /f/AI/finflow/.env.local <worktree>/.env.local` (never print it) and `npm ci`, from the worktree root. Chain C's Task 11 Step 1 then adds `EB_STATE_SECRET` to the **worktree's** `.env.local`, and Task 14 must run in that same worktree (its probe runner reads `.env.local` from the cwd). Rebase the chains onto `s00-platform` one after the other. Then, in the main tree, make sure `F:/AI/finflow/.env.local` has `EB_STATE_SECRET` (copy the line over from chain C's worktree if `grep -c '^EB_STATE_SECRET=' .env.local` prints `0`; never print it), and run `npm ci && npm run test:unit && npm run typecheck && npm run lint` before Task 20.
- Task 23 (E4) Steps 1–10 (create the Vercel project, upload the env vars, ask the user for the Enable Banking Application ID) need only Task 2's `.env.local` and the `.secrets/` files. They can start any time after Task 2, which also gets the [USER] request of Step 8 out early. Its commit (Step 11) must land on `s00-platform`, and its Step 12 (commit author) must be done, before Task 24 commits.
- Task 28 (E7) can run in parallel with Tasks 26–27 once Task 25 Step 9 shows a green `ci` run on `master`.

**Waiting on the user.** Task 15 Step 1 (local Java, only if no Java 21 is installed); Task 23 Step 6 (only on a GitHub access error), Step 8 and Step 12; Task 25 Step 11; Task 26 Step 11; Task 27 Steps 2 and 5; Task 29 Steps 3 and 6.

## Part A: Repo cleanup + remove Cloudflare/OpenNext + Next.js 16 upgrade

This part delivers spec section 1 (platform) and the cleanup and documentation bullets of section 3. The repo loses its unrelated files and the false landing-page claims. Cloudflare Pages / OpenNext / wrangler go away completely, and the app moves from Next.js 15.5 to **Next.js 16.4.2** (React 19.2.9, `eslint-config-next` 16.4.2, `middleware.ts` → `proxy.ts`, Turbopack for dev and build), with `npm run lint` / `npm run typecheck` scripts that pass. **Preconditions:** none for A1–A3. They run first in S00, on a clean `master` checkout, and A1 creates the S00 working branch `s00-platform` that every later part commits to. **A4 (docs) is Task 24:** it runs after Part E Task E4 and right before Task E5 pushes `s00-platform`, because it documents the files of every other part and has to be the last commit in the S00 PR.

Every command, expected output and code snippet below was dry-run on 2026-10-22 against a clone of `master` at `b68ecca` (npm 11.11, Node 24.14.1, Windows / Git Bash). The trial covered `npm ci`, the edits, `tsc`, `eslint`, `next build` on 15.5.14 and on 16.4.2, `next dev`, `next start` and the HTTP smoke script.

**Conventions for this part**

- Shell: Git Bash (POSIX sh) on the Windows host, cwd `F:/AI/finflow`. Every command block starts with `cd /f/AI/finflow || exit 1` (plus the repo check before any write), because shell state does not persist between tool calls and the session's default cwd is another repo (see **How to read this plan**).
- Line endings: `core.autocrlf=true`, so the working tree is CRLF and the index is LF. `warning: LF will be replaced by CRLF ...` from git is expected and harmless. An exact-match edit tool must match the CRLF text, so the before/after snippets below are given without any trailing whitespace.
- Never print a secret value. `.env`, `.env.local` and `.secrets/` are gitignored. The public Firebase web config in `wrangler.toml` is copied by a script into `.env.local` (A2 Step 1), never into a tracked file.
- After A2, the public `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` values exist **only** in the untracked `F:/AI/finflow/.env.local` (and in git history: `git show b68ecca:wrangler.toml`). The Vercel environment-variables task (Task E4) reads them from there. CI does not: it uses dummy `demo-finflow` values.
- Do not push in this part. The CI part opens the (draft) PR from `s00-platform` in Task E3 Step 6.

```bash
cd /f/AI/finflow || exit 1
[ "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" = "F:/AI/finflow" ] || { echo WRONG REPO; exit 1; }
```

Expected:
- `git status --short` prints nothing, or only `?? docs/superpowers/plans/` (this plan, if it has not been committed yet). If it prints anything else, stop and ask the user. Do not stash or discard their work.
- The branch is `master` and the last commit is `b68ecca docs: Finmap reverse-engineering report, roadmap and S00 platform spec` (or a later docs-only commit).
- `Switched to a new branch 's00-platform'`.

If the S00 plan is untracked, commit it first so that every later part starts from a clean tree. Commit only the plan file itself. If drafts such as `docs/superpowers/plans/parts/` exist, they repeat the plan with older numbering: delete them, never commit them.

```bash
cd /f/AI/finflow || exit 1
[ "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" = "F:/AI/finflow" ] || { echo WRONG REPO; exit 1; }
rm -rf docs/superpowers/plans/parts
git status --short | grep -v '^?? docs/superpowers/plans/' || true
git add docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-22-s00-platform.md
git diff --cached --quiet || git commit -m "docs: S00 implementation plan" -m "Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
git status --short
```

Expected: the first `git status` line prints nothing, the commit (if one was needed) reports `[s00-platform <sha>] docs: S00 implementation plan` and `1 file changed`, and the final `git status --short` prints nothing. If it still lists `?? docs/superpowers/plans/<something>`, stop and ask the user what that file is.

Then install dependencies (node_modules is not committed, and this checkout has none):

```bash
cd /f/AI/finflow || exit 1
[ "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" = "F:/AI/finflow" ] || { echo WRONG REPO; exit 1; }
npm ci
```

Create `.env.example`:

```dotenv
# FinFlow environment variables: NAMES ONLY. Never commit values.
# Local dev: values live in .env.local (gitignored).
# Vercel: plain variables below are "plain"; the server-only ones are "sensitive".

# --- Public (plain): inlined into the browser bundle at build time ---
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_API_KEY=
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_AUTH_DOMAIN=
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID=
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_STORAGE_BUCKET=
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_MESSAGING_SENDER_ID=
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_APP_ID=
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_MEASUREMENT_ID=
# Public origin of the app (Enable Banking redirect URL, robots, sitemap)
NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL=

# --- Sensitive (server-only) ---
# Enable Banking application id and its RSA private key (PKCS#8 PEM; newlines may be written as \n)
ENABLEBANKING_APP_ID=
ENABLEBANKING_PRIVATE_KEY=
# base64 of the Firebase service-account JSON
FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT=
# 32 random bytes, base64 (HMAC key for the Enable Banking OAuth state)
EB_STATE_SECRET=

# --- Local emulators / CI only (never set in Vercel) ---
# NEXT_PUBLIC_USE_EMULATORS=1
# FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST=127.0.0.1:8080
# FIREBASE_AUTH_EMULATOR_HOST=127.0.0.1:9099

# --- Tooling only: these live in .env (gitignored), never in .env.local or Vercel ---
# VERCEL_TOKEN=
# VERCEL_TEAM_ID=
# FIREBASE_ACCOUNT=   (Firebase CLI account passed as --account to finflow-e94b5 commands)
```

### Task 2: Remove Cloudflare Pages / OpenNext / wrangler (A2)

**Files:**
- Create (untracked, gitignored): `.env.local` from the public `[vars]` of `wrangler.toml`
- Delete: `wrangler.toml` (lines 1–14), `open-next.config.ts` (lines 1–3), `scripts/patch-cf.js` (lines 1–64; `scripts/` becomes empty and disappears)
- Modify: `package.json`: line 8 (`build:cf` script) via `npm pkg delete`; devDependencies lines 32 and 42 (`@opennextjs/cloudflare`, `wrangler`) via `npm uninstall`; adds `dependencies.server-only` via `npm install`
- Modify: `package-lock.json` (npm only)
- Modify: `next.config.ts`: full rewrite of lines 1–45
- Modify: `lib/enablebanking/jwt.ts`: full rewrite of lines 1–28
- Modify: `.gitignore`: lines 37–45 (OpenNext / Wrangler blocks between `.vercel` and `# typescript`)
- Test: `createJwt` check with a throwaway RSA key (Node 24 type stripping), `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (still Next 15, webpack), HTTP smoke on `next dev`

Why each change: the spec requires zero OpenNext / wrangler / `getCloudflareContext` in code and dependencies. The webpack aliases forced the *browser* build of `@firebase/firestore` into the server bundle only to dodge `new Function()` in Workers, so on Node they are pointless. `serverExternalPackages: ['firebase-admin']` is redundant: `firebase-admin` is already on Next's built-in external list (`node_modules/next/dist/lib/server-external-packages.json` line 34 in 15.5, `.jsonc` line 44 in 16.4). The `eslint.ignoreDuringBuilds` block stays until A3, because removing it on Next 15 would make `next build` lint (and fail on the existing errors), and on Next 16 the option no longer exists.

- [ ] **Step 1: Copy the public `[vars]` of `wrangler.toml` into `.env.local` (before deleting it)**

```bash
cd /f/AI/finflow || exit 1
[ "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" = "F:/AI/finflow" ] || { echo WRONG REPO; exit 1; }
if [ -e .env.local ]; then T=.env.local.from-wrangler; echo "NOTE: .env.local exists - writing $T, merge missing keys by hand"; else T=.env.local; fi
awk '/^\[vars\]/{f=1;next} /^\[/{f=0} f && /=/ { sub(/\r$/, ""); sub(/[ \t]*=[ \t]*/, "="); gsub(/"/, ""); print }' wrangler.toml > "$T"
sed -i 's#^NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL=.*#NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL=http://localhost:3000#' "$T"
awk -F= '{ printf "%s (value length %d)\n", $1, length($2) }' "$T"
git check-ignore -v "$T"
```

Expected (names and lengths only, never values):

```
NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL (value length 21)
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_API_KEY (value length 39)
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_AUTH_DOMAIN (value length 29)
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID (value length 13)
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_STORAGE_BUCKET (value length 33)
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_MESSAGING_SENDER_ID (value length 12)
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_APP_ID (value length 41)
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_MEASUREMENT_ID (value length 12)
.gitignore:34:.env*	.env.local
```

`NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` is set to `http://localhost:3000` for local dev. The production value comes from Vercel. Enable Banking variables are not added here: without them, `/api/enablebanking/*` answers 500 "not configured" locally, which the smoke check expects. `.env` (tooling tokens) is not touched.

- [ ] **Step 6: Rewrite `lib/enablebanking/jwt.ts` (full file, replaces lines 1–28)**

```ts
import 'server-only'
import { SignJWT, importPKCS8 } from 'jose'

/**
 * Signs the short-lived RS256 JWT that authenticates FinFlow to the Enable Banking API.
 * Claims follow https://enablebanking.com/docs/api/reference/ ("JWT format and signature"):
 * header { typ: 'JWT', alg: 'RS256', kid: <application id> },
 * body { iss: 'enablebanking.com', aud: 'api.enablebanking.com', iat, exp } (TTL at most 24 h).
 * ENABLEBANKING_PRIVATE_KEY is a PKCS#8 PEM; literal "\n" sequences are turned into newlines
 * so the key can be stored as a single-line environment variable.
 */
export async function createJwt(): Promise<string> {
  const appId = process.env.ENABLEBANKING_APP_ID
  const rawKey = process.env.ENABLEBANKING_PRIVATE_KEY
  if (!appId || !rawKey) {
    throw new Error('Enable Banking is not configured: set ENABLEBANKING_APP_ID and ENABLEBANKING_PRIVATE_KEY')
  }
  const privateKey = await importPKCS8(rawKey.replace(/\\n/g, '\n'), 'RS256')
  const now = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000)
  return new SignJWT({})
    .setProtectedHeader({ alg: 'RS256', typ: 'JWT', kid: appId })
    .setIssuer('enablebanking.com')
    .setIssuedAt(now)
    .setAudience('api.enablebanking.com')
    .setExpirationTime(now + 3600)
    .sign(privateKey)
}
```

The JWT claims now follow the Enable Banking API reference ("JWT format and signature": header `typ: "JWT"`, `alg: "RS256"`, `kid: <application_id>`; body `iss: "enablebanking.com"` and `aud: "api.enablebanking.com"`, both "always the same", plus `iat`/`exp` with a TTL of at most 24 h). The old file sent no `typ` and put the app id into `iss`; jose's `SignJWT` adds no `typ` by itself. The 1 h expiry and RS256 stay. Task 23 Step 13 checks the claims against the live API before the PR.

- [ ] **Step 9: Create the HTTP smoke script (outside the repo, reused in A3)**

It starts the server itself, checks the landing claims, the proxy redirects, the COOP header and the Enable Banking env wiring, then kills the server's process tree. It does not use `curl`. Write it to `/tmp/finflow-smoke.sh` (Git Bash maps `/tmp` to `%TEMP%`):

```bash
cd /f/AI/finflow || exit 1
[ "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" = "F:/AI/finflow" ] || { echo WRONG REPO; exit 1; }
cat > /tmp/finflow-smoke.sh <<'SMOKE'
#!/bin/sh
# FinFlow HTTP smoke check (S00 Part A). Run from the repo root:
#   sh /tmp/finflow-smoke.sh dev     -> starts `next dev`   on :3100, checks, stops it
#   sh /tmp/finflow-smoke.sh start   -> starts `next start` on :3101 (needs `npm run build` first)
node --input-type=module - "${1:-dev}" <<'EOF'
import { spawn, execSync } from 'node:child_process'
import { openSync } from 'node:fs'
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os'
import { join } from 'node:path'

const mode = process.argv[2] === 'start' ? 'start' : 'dev'
const port = mode === 'dev' ? '3100' : '3101'
const base = `http://localhost:${port}`
const logFile = join(tmpdir(), `finflow-smoke-${mode}.log`)
const out = openSync(logFile, 'w')
const server = spawn(process.execPath, ['node_modules/next/dist/bin/next', mode, '-p', port], {
  env: { ...process.env, NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED: '1' },
  detached: process.platform !== 'win32',
  stdio: ['ignore', out, out],
})
const stop = () => {
  try {
    if (process.platform === 'win32') execSync(`taskkill /PID ${server.pid} /T /F`, { stdio: 'ignore' })
    else process.kill(-server.pid, 'SIGTERM')
  } catch {}
}
const get = (path, headers = {}) => fetch(base + path, { redirect: 'manual', headers })
let failed = 0
const check = (name, ok, detail) => {
  if (!ok) failed++
  console.log(`${ok ? 'PASS' : 'FAIL'} ${name}${ok ? '' : ' :: ' + detail}`)
}
try {
  for (let i = 0; ; i++) {
    try { await get('/'); break } catch { if (i >= 120) throw new Error('server did not start in 120 s') }
    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 1000))
  }
  let r = await get('/')
  let body = await r.text()
  check('GET / is 200', r.status === 200, r.status)
  check('COOP header kept', r.headers.get('cross-origin-opener-policy') === 'same-origin-allow-popups', r.headers.get('cross-origin-opener-policy'))
  for (const s of ['Що кажуть клієнти', '2 400+', '94%', 'Авторуль', 'Командний доступ', 'Автоматична категоризація']) {
    check(`landing has no "${s}"`, !body.includes(s), 'still present')
  }
  r = await get('/pricing')
  body = await r.text()
  check('GET /pricing is 200', r.status === 200, r.status)
  check('pricing has no team / auto-categorisation claims', !body.includes('До 5 учасників') && !body.includes('Автоматична категоризація'), 'still present')
  r = await get('/abc/transactions')
  check('proxy: anonymous /abc/transactions -> /login', r.status === 307 && (r.headers.get('location') ?? '').includes('/login?redirect=%2Fabc%2Ftransactions'), `${r.status} ${r.headers.get('location')}`)
  r = await get('/login', { cookie: '__session=1' })
  check('proxy: /login with __session -> /', r.status === 307 && new URL(r.headers.get('location') ?? '/x', base).pathname === '/', `${r.status} ${r.headers.get('location')}`)
  r = await get('/api/enablebanking/institutions?country=PL')
  body = await r.text()
  const ebOk = (r.status === 500 && body.includes('Enable Banking is not configured')) || r.status === 200 || r.status === 401
  check('EB route reads process.env (500 "not configured" without EB vars; 200 with them; 401 once Bearer auth landed)', ebOk, `${r.status} ${body.slice(0, 160)}`)
} catch (e) {
  failed++
  console.log('FAIL', e.message)
} finally {
  stop()
  console.log(failed === 0 ? 'SMOKE OK' : `SMOKE FAILED (${failed}) - server log: ${logFile}`)
  process.exit(failed === 0 ? 0 : 1)
}
EOF
SMOKE
echo written
```

Expected: `written`. Ports 3100/3101 must be free. If a check fails, the last line names the server log file.

### Task 3: Upgrade to Next.js 16 (proxy.ts, Turbopack, lint/typecheck gates) (A3)

**Files:**
- Modify: `package.json` / `package-lock.json`: `next` `^15.1.0` → `^16.4.2`, `react`/`react-dom` `19.2.4` → `19.2.9`, `eslint-config-next` `16.2.2` → `16.4.2`; scripts `lint` = `eslint .`, `typecheck` = `tsc --noEmit`; `engines.node` = `24.x`
- Rename: `middleware.ts` → `proxy.ts`, with line 7 `export function middleware(` → `export function proxy(` (logic unchanged), done by the official `middleware-to-proxy` codemod
- Modify: `next.config.ts`: drop the `eslint` block (lines 4–8 after A2)
- Modify: `tsconfig.json`: line 18, `"jsx": "preserve"` → `"jsx": "react-jsx"` (mandatory in Next 16; `next build` rewrites it otherwise)
- Modify: `eslint.config.mjs`: full rewrite of lines 1–18
- Modify: `components/charts/BalanceSheet.tsx`: lines 228 and 244 (apostrophes)
- Modify: `components/charts/CashFlowChart.tsx`: lines 28–34 (typed tooltip props instead of `any`)
- Create: `AGENTS.md` (written by `next dev` 16.4; committed as is)
- Test: `next-async-request-api` codemod dry run, `middleware-to-proxy` codemod diff check, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` (FAIL → PASS), `npm run build`, smoke on `next start` and `next dev`

**What the official Next 16 upgrade guide requires, and how this app is affected** (https://nextjs.org/docs/app/guides/upgrading/version-16, "Last updated October 15, 2026"):

| Breaking change | This repo |
|---|---|
| Node ≥ 20.9, TypeScript ≥ 5.1 | Node 24.14, TS 5.9.3. OK. TypeScript stays on `^5`: the `typescript` package's `latest` tag is now 7.x, which we do not adopt here. |
| Sync `params` / `searchParams` / `cookies()` / `headers()` / `draftMode()` removed | The only server-side use is `app/(app)/[orgSlug]/analytics/page.tsx`, which already does `await params`. All other pages are client components that use `useParams()`/`useSearchParams()`, and those are unaffected. The route handlers read `req.nextUrl`. The codemod dry run (Step 1) confirms `0 ok`. |
| `middleware` → `proxy` (file + exported function; `proxy` runtime is `nodejs`, edge not allowed) | `middleware.ts` uses no `runtime` export, so the change is a rename of the file and the exported function. Step 3 runs the official `npx @next/codemod middleware-to-proxy` and checks that this is all it changed. No `skipMiddlewareUrlNormalize`-style flags exist in `next.config.ts`. |
| Turbopack is the default for `next dev` and `next build`, and a custom `webpack` config **fails** the build | The `webpack()` block was removed in A2, so no `--webpack` flag is needed. There are no Sass/`~` imports. |
| `next lint` removed, `eslint` key removed from the Next config, `next build` no longer lints | The `lint` script becomes `eslint .`. Leaving the `eslint` key is a **type error** under 16 (`TS2353 ... 'eslint' does not exist in type 'NextConfig'`, seen in the dry run), so Step 4 removes it. |
| `next/image` defaults (`imageSizes`, `qualities`, local `?query` needs `localPatterns`), `images.domains` deprecated | `next/image` is not used anywhere. |
| AMP, `serverRuntimeConfig`/`publicRuntimeConfig`, `experimental.dynamicIO`/`ppr`, `devIndicators` options, `unstable_rootParams` removed; parallel routes need `default.js` | None of these is used. |
| `revalidateTag(tag, profile)` signature | Not used. |
| Scroll-behavior override and `next dev` writing to `.next/dev` | No global smooth scroll. `.next/**` is already lint-ignored and gitignored. |

**Why not `npx @next/codemod upgrade latest`:** the dry run with `@next/codemod@16.4.2 upgrade 16.4.2 --yes` also ran `cache-components-instant-false`. That codemod inserted `export const instant = false;` plus a 3-line `@next-codemod-ignore` comment into 6 server files (`app/layout.tsx`, `app/page.tsx`, `app/pricing/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/layout.tsx`, `app/(app)/[orgSlug]/analytics/{layout,page}.tsx`). The upgrade guide's own checklist says Cache Components must not be adopted just to finish the upgrade. The upgrade codemod also pins `eslint` to an exact version. So we install pinned versions manually, which the guide documents as an alternative ("Install packages manually"), and run only the codemods that apply: `next-async-request-api` as a dry run (Step 1, nothing to migrate) and `middleware-to-proxy` for real (Step 3).

```js
import { defineConfig, globalIgnores } from "eslint/config";
import nextVitals from "eslint-config-next/core-web-vitals";
import nextTs from "eslint-config-next/typescript";

const eslintConfig = defineConfig([
  ...nextVitals,
  ...nextTs,

]);

export default eslintConfig;
```

- [ ] **Step 11: Type the Recharts tooltip in `components/charts/CashFlowChart.tsx` (lines 28–34)**

Replace:

```tsx
// eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any
function CustomTooltip({ active, payload, label }: any) {
  if (!active || !payload?.length) return null

  const income = (payload.find((p: any) => p.dataKey === 'income')?.value as number) ?? 0
  const expense = (payload.find((p: any) => p.dataKey === 'expense')?.value as number) ?? 0
  const net = (income as number) - (expense as number)
```

with:

```tsx
interface TooltipEntry {
  dataKey?: string | number
  value?: number | string
}

interface CustomTooltipProps {
  active?: boolean
  payload?: TooltipEntry[]
  label?: string | number
}

function CustomTooltip({ active, payload, label }: CustomTooltipProps) {
  if (!active || !payload?.length) return null

  const income = Number(payload.find((p) => p.dataKey === 'income')?.value ?? 0)
  const expense = Number(payload.find((p) => p.dataKey === 'expense')?.value ?? 0)
  const net = income - expense
```

The usage `<Tooltip content={<CustomTooltip />} />` (line 103) stays unchanged. Every prop is optional, and Recharts injects them at render time.

| Next 16 peer `react ^18.2 \|\| ^19`, engines `node >=20.9.0` | `npm view next@16.4.2 peerDependencies engines` |
| Upgrade guide: codemod list, async request APIs, `middleware`→`proxy` (+ `proxy` = nodejs only), Turbopack default and webpack-config build failure, `next lint` + `eslint` config key removed, `next/image` defaults, removals, `.next/dev` | https://nextjs.org/docs/app/guides/upgrading/version-16 |
| `upgrade` codemod options (`--yes`, `--skip-react-upgrade`), `middleware-to-proxy` behaviour, `next-async-request-api` | https://nextjs.org/docs/app/guides/upgrading/codemods |
| `@next/codemod@16.4.2 middleware-to-proxy` refuses a dirty tree without `--force`, renames `middleware.ts` → `proxy.ts` on disk (unstaged) and changes only `export function middleware(` → `export function proxy(` | run on a clone of `b68ecca` |
| `firebase-admin` is on Next's built-in `serverExternalPackages` list | `node_modules/next/dist/lib/server-external-packages.json(c)` in 15.5.14 and 16.4.2 |
| `next dev` 16.4 writes `AGENTS.md` (only that file; disable with `agentRules: false`) | `node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/generate-agent-files.js`, dev-server log |
| `engines.node` overrides the Vercel project Node version | https://vercel.com/docs/functions/runtimes/node-js/node-js-versions |
| Only server-side request-API usage is `analytics/page.tsx` (already `await params`); no `cookies()`/`headers()`; no `next/image` | `git grep` + codemod dry run (`0 ok`) |
| Lint baseline under `eslint-config-next` 16.4.2: 15 errors (11 React Compiler rules, 2 `no-unescaped-entities`, 2 `no-explicit-any`); 0 errors after Steps 9–11 | dry run |

## Part B: Vitest harness + firebase-admin + server auth helpers + /api/health

This part delivers spec section 2. It adds the Vitest unit-test harness, including a `server-only` stub. It adds the trusted server layer in `lib/server/`: `HttpError`/`errorResponse`, a lazily initialised `firebase-admin` v14 singleton, and the bearer-token and org-membership guards. It also adds `GET /api/health`. Every piece is unit-tested with the Admin SDK mocked, so the local machine needs no emulator, Java or credentials.

**Preconditions:** Part A Tasks A1–A3 are complete (A4, the docs task, runs later as Task 24). That means Next 16 is installed, Cloudflare/OpenNext is removed, `npm ci` has run, the `lint` (`eslint .`) and `typecheck` (`tsc --noEmit`) scripts exist, and both pass. Part C (`eb-state`, Enable Banking routes) and the CI/e2e part build on the exports created here.

**Facts this part relies on (verified 2026-10-22, see the notes at the end):**

- `vitest@5.0.5` is the latest release. It needs Node `^22.12 || ^24`. It lists `vite` as a required peer, which npm installs automatically (`vite@8.x`). It also lists `@types/node` `^22 || >=24` as an optional peer. With the repo's current `@types/node@^20`, `npm install vitest` fails with `ERESOLVE`, so this part bumps `@types/node` to `^24`, which matches Node 24.
- `firebase-admin@14.5.0` (Node `>=22`, modular API only; v14 removed the legacy namespace) exports the following:
  - `firebase-admin/app`: `getApps(): App[]`, `initializeApp(options?, name?)`, `cert(serviceAccountPathOrObject)`. `cert` accepts the downloaded snake_case JSON (`project_id`, `private_key`, `client_email`).
  - `firebase-admin/firestore`: `initializeFirestore(app, { preferRest?: boolean }, databaseId?)`, type `Firestore`.
  - `firebase-admin/auth`: `getAuth(app?)`, type `Auth`, `auth.verifyIdToken(idToken, checkRevoked?) → Promise<DecodedIdToken>` with `.uid`.
- If no credential is passed, `initializeApp({ projectId })` falls back to lazy Application Default Credentials and does not throw at init. With `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` set, Firestore then talks to the emulator without credentials **only over gRPC** (the SDK sends its built-in `Bearer owner` emulator token). With `preferRest: true` the REST transport still loads Application Default Credentials and every read fails with `Error: Could not load the default credentials` (verified with firebase-admin 14.5.0 against the Firestore emulator: `preferRest: true` fails, `preferRest: false` reads fine). So `getAdmin()` uses `preferRest` only outside the emulator. The unit tests mock the SDK and cannot see this; the e2e `GET /api/health` on the emulators (Task 21) does.
- `server-only@0.0.1` resolves to an empty module only under the `react-server` export condition, which Next.js uses. Under Vitest it resolves to `index.js`, which **throws** on import, so tests alias it to an empty stub.
- In Next 16, `export const runtime = 'nodejs'` and `export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` are valid route-segment config as long as Cache Components is **not** enabled. `/api/*` is excluded from the existing middleware/proxy matcher (`'/((?!_next/static|_next/image|favicon.ico|api/).*)'`), so `/api/health` is never redirected to `/login`.

**Error messages other parts can rely on:** 401 → `{ "error": "unauthorized" }`, 403 → `{ "error": "forbidden" }`, missing service account → 500 `{ "error": "server not configured" }`, anything unexpected → 500 `{ "error": "internal" }`.

All commands below run in **Git Bash** with cwd `F:/AI/finflow`: every block starts with `cd /f/AI/finflow || exit 1`, and blocks that install, write or commit also run the repo check from **How to read this plan**. Create and edit files with absolute paths (`F:/AI/finflow/...`). Commit on the working branch that Part A put you on. Part B never creates, switches or pushes branches.

- [ ] **Step 8: Create the `server-only` stub**

Create `tests/unit/server-only-stub.ts`:

```ts
// Test-only stand-in for the `server-only` package.
// The real package throws on import unless the bundler resolves the
// `react-server` export condition (Next.js does; Vitest does not).
// vitest.config.ts aliases `server-only` to this empty module.
export {}
```

- [ ] **Step 9: Create the Vitest config**

Create `vitest.config.ts`:

```ts
import { resolve } from 'node:path'
import { defineConfig } from 'vitest/config'

const root = import.meta.dirname

export default defineConfig({
  resolve: {
    alias: {
      // Same as tsconfig "paths": "@/*" -> "./*"
      '@': root,
      // `import 'server-only'` in lib/server/** must not throw under Vitest
      'server-only': resolve(root, 'tests/unit/server-only-stub.ts'),
    },
  },
  test: {
    environment: 'node',
    include: ['tests/unit/**/*.test.ts'],
    // Undo vi.spyOn(...) and vi.stubEnv(...) after every test.
    restoreMocks: true,
    unstubEnvs: true,
  },
})
```

```bash
npm run test:unit

```

```
      Tests  2 passed (2)
```

Vite also prints a yellow notice, `(!) Your Vite config uses features that are unsupported by configLoader: 'native' ... ESM syntax in a file loaded as CommonJS (vitest.config.ts:1:1)`. This is expected and harmless. `package.json` has no `"type": "module"`, and the default `bundle` config loader handles the `.ts` file fine. Do **not** rename the file or change `"type"`, because other parts reference `vitest.config.ts`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

Create `lib/server/http-error.ts`. It has no `server-only` import because it is pure and safe anywhere.

```ts
/**
 * An error that maps 1:1 onto an HTTP response.
 * Throw it from server code; route handlers turn it into JSON via errorResponse().
 */
export class HttpError extends Error {
  constructor(
    public readonly status: number,
    message: string,
  ) {
    super(message)
    this.name = 'HttpError'
  }
}

/**
 * Converts anything thrown inside a route handler into a JSON Response.
 * HttpError -> { error: message } with its status.
 * Anything else -> logged server-side, client gets a generic 500 (no internals leak).
 */
export function errorResponse(e: unknown): Response {
  if (e instanceof HttpError) {
    return Response.json({ error: e.message }, { status: e.status })
  }
  console.error(e)
  return Response.json({ error: 'internal' }, { status: 500 })
}
```

**Files:**

The SDK itself is not worth unit-testing. The real Admin SDK is exercised end to end by `/api/health` on Vercel and by the e2e run against the emulators. What breaks in practice is **our env branching**: the emulator project vs the service account, the "missing secret" error, `preferRest` (REST in production, gRPC on the emulator), and the singleton. Testing that with `firebase-admin/app|firestore|auth` mocked is cheap (6 tests, no network), so we do it.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

Create `lib/server/firebase-admin.ts`:

```ts
import 'server-only'
import { cert, getApps, initializeApp, type App } from 'firebase-admin/app'
import { getAuth, type Auth } from 'firebase-admin/auth'
import { initializeFirestore, type Firestore } from 'firebase-admin/firestore'
import { HttpError } from '@/lib/server/http-error'

type Admin = { db: Firestore; auth: Auth }

let cached: Admin | null = null

function createApp(): App {
  // Emulator (CI e2e, local emulators): firebase emulators:exec sets
  // FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST and FIREBASE_AUTH_EMULATOR_HOST; the SDK talks to
  // them without credentials, it only needs a project id.
  if (process.env.FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST) {
    return initializeApp({
      projectId: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID || 'demo-finflow',
    })
  }

  // Production / preview: base64 of the service-account JSON downloaded from
  // Firebase Console (keeps the PEM newlines intact inside one env var).
  const encoded = process.env.FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT
  if (!encoded) {
    throw new HttpError(500, 'server not configured')
  }
  const serviceAccount = JSON.parse(Buffer.from(encoded, 'base64').toString('utf8'))
  return initializeApp({ credential: cert(serviceAccount) })
}

/**
 * Lazily initialised firebase-admin singleton. Server-only.
 * getApps() lets a dev hot reload reuse the already-initialised default app.
 */
export function getAdmin(): { db: Firestore; auth: Auth } {
  if (cached) return cached
  const app = getApps()[0] ?? createApp()
  cached = {
    // Production: REST transport (no gRPC channel setup, faster serverless cold start).
    // Emulator: gRPC, because REST loads Application Default Credentials even there
    // and fails with "Could not load the default credentials".
    db: initializeFirestore(app, { preferRest: !process.env.FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST }),
    auth: getAuth(app),
  }
  return cached
}
```

Why `preferRest` depends on the emulator: the REST transport always loads Application Default Credentials, also against `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST`, and the emulator branch has none, so every read would fail with `Could not load the default credentials` (`GET /api/health` → 500 in the e2e run). gRPC talks to the emulator with the SDK's built-in emulator token. Production keeps REST for the faster cold start.

Why the `getApps()[0] ?? createApp()` dance: each route handler is its own server bundle, and dev hot reload re-evaluates modules, so the module-level `cached` can be empty while the SDK's default app already exists. With a credential, a second `initializeApp()` throws `Firebase app named "[DEFAULT]" already exists and initializeApp was invoked with an optional Credential ... Please use getApp or getApps to reuse the existing app instead.` (verified in `firebase-admin@14.5.0` `lib/app/lifecycle.js`). `initializeFirestore()` on the same app with the same settings returns the existing instance (verified in `FirestoreService.initializeDatabase`); within one process `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` does not change, so the settings are always the same.

Create `lib/server/auth.ts`:

```ts
import 'server-only'

/**
 * Pure membership check against an organizations/{orgId} document's data.
 * Mirrors the Firestore rule `request.auth.uid in resource.data.members`.
 */
export function isOrgMember(orgData: unknown, uid: string): boolean {
  if (!uid || typeof orgData !== 'object' || orgData === null) return false
  const members: unknown = (orgData as { members?: unknown }).members
  if (typeof members !== 'object' || members === null || Array.isArray(members)) return false
  return Object.hasOwn(members, uid)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Implement `requireUserFromBearer`**

Replace the whole content of `lib/server/auth.ts` with:

```ts
import 'server-only'
import { getAdmin } from '@/lib/server/firebase-admin'
import { HttpError } from '@/lib/server/http-error'

// RFC 7235: the auth scheme is case-insensitive; the token is one opaque word.
const BEARER = /^Bearer (\S+)$/i

/**
 * Pure membership check against an organizations/{orgId} document's data.
 * Mirrors the Firestore rule `request.auth.uid in resource.data.members`.
 */
export function isOrgMember(orgData: unknown, uid: string): boolean {
  if (!uid || typeof orgData !== 'object' || orgData === null) return false
  const members: unknown = (orgData as { members?: unknown }).members
  if (typeof members !== 'object' || members === null || Array.isArray(members)) return false
  return Object.hasOwn(members, uid)
}

/**
 * Reads `Authorization: Bearer <Firebase ID token>` and verifies it with
 * revocation checking. Throws HttpError(401) for a missing/malformed header or
 * any token the Admin SDK rejects. Temporary until S01 session cookies.
 */
export async function requireUserFromBearer(req: Request): Promise<{ uid: string }> {
  const match = BEARER.exec(req.headers.get('authorization') ?? '')
  if (!match) throw new HttpError(401, 'unauthorized')

  // Outside the try: a missing service account must surface as 500, not 401.
  const { auth } = getAdmin()
  try {
    const decoded = await auth.verifyIdToken(match[1], true)
    return { uid: decoded.uid }
  } catch {
    throw new HttpError(401, 'unauthorized')
  }
}
```

```ts
    admin.get.mockResolvedValue(orgSnapshot({ ownerId: 'u2', members: { u2: { role: 'owner' } } }))

    const err = await requireOrgMember('u1', 'org1').catch((e: unknown) => e)

    expect(err).toBeInstanceOf(HttpError)
    expect(err).toMatchObject({ status: 403, message: 'forbidden' })
  })

  it.each([
    ['an empty orgId', ''],
    ['an orgId that is a path', 'org1/accounts/acc1'],
  ])('rejects %s with 403 without reading Firestore', async (_label, orgId) => {
    const err = await requireOrgMember('u1', orgId).catch((e: unknown) => e)

    expect(err).toMatchObject({ status: 403, message: 'forbidden' })
    expect(admin.doc).not.toHaveBeenCalled()
  })

  it('lets Firestore failures propagate (they become 500, not 403)', async () => {
    const outage = new Error('UNAVAILABLE')
    admin.get.mockRejectedValue(outage)

    await expect(requireOrgMember('u1', 'org1')).rejects.toBe(outage)
  })
})
```

- [ ] **Step 11: Implement `requireOrgMember`**

Append this block to the **end** of `lib/server/auth.ts`. Nothing else in the file changes. The final file is the Step 7 content followed by this block:

```ts

/**
 * Throws HttpError(403) unless organizations/{orgId} exists and lists uid in
 * its `members` map. Reads with the Admin SDK (bypasses security rules).
 * Temporary until S02 moves membership to its own document.
 */
export async function requireOrgMember(uid: string, orgId: string): Promise<void> {
  // orgId comes from the request: refuse anything that is not a single doc id,
  // otherwise "org1/accounts/x" would read a different document.
  if (!orgId || orgId.includes('/')) throw new HttpError(403, 'forbidden')

  const snap = await getAdmin().db.doc(`organizations/${orgId}`).get()
  if (!snap.exists || !isOrgMember(snap.data(), uid)) {
    throw new HttpError(403, 'forbidden')
  }
}
```

(The Admin SDK's `DocumentSnapshot.exists` is a boolean **property**, unlike the client SDK's `exists()` method.)

Create `app/api/health/route.ts`:

```ts
import { getAdmin } from '@/lib/server/firebase-admin'
import { errorResponse } from '@/lib/server/http-error'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'
export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'

// GET /api/health — proves the function can reach Firestore with the Admin SDK.
// Reads one service document (it does not need to exist) and returns no user data.
export async function GET(): Promise<Response> {
  try {
    await getAdmin().db.doc('_meta/health').get()
    return Response.json({
      ok: true,
      region: process.env.VERCEL_REGION ?? 'local',
      firestore: 'ok',
    })
  } catch (e) {
    return errorResponse(e)
  }
}
```

Notes:
- `_meta/health` does not need to exist. Reading a missing document still proves credentials, network and IAM.
- Do not create `_meta/health` from the client. The Part D rules (Task D2) deny all client access to `_meta/**`, and the Admin SDK ignores rules.
- If Cache Components (`cacheComponents: true`) is ever enabled in `next.config.ts`, delete the `dynamic` export. Next 16 rejects `dynamic` in that mode, and GET handlers are dynamic by default anyway.

This step makes one read-only Admin SDK call, a `get` of the non-existent doc `_meta/health` in `finflow-e94b5`, using the service-account key in `.secrets/`. The key value is never printed. Skip it if you do not want local code touching production. The same check runs on Vercel in the deploy part (acceptance criterion 2).

An agent has no second terminal, so one script starts `next dev` on port 3103 with the service account passed in, asks `/api/health` once, prints the answer and kills the server's process tree (the same pattern as `/tmp/finflow-smoke.sh` in Part A):

```bash
cd /f/AI/finflow || exit 1
SA_FILE="$(ls .secrets/finflow-e94b5-firebase-adminsdk-*.json)" node --input-type=module - <<'EOF'
import { execSync, spawn } from 'node:child_process'
import { openSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs'
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os'
import { join } from 'node:path'

const port = 3103
const logFile = join(tmpdir(), 'finflow-health-dev.log')
const out = openSync(logFile, 'w')
const server = spawn(process.execPath, ['node_modules/next/dist/bin/next', 'dev', '-p', String(port)], {
  env: {
    ...process.env,
    NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED: '1',
    FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT: readFileSync(process.env.SA_FILE).toString('base64'),
  },
  detached: process.platform !== 'win32',
  stdio: ['ignore', out, out],
})
try {
  let res = null
  for (let i = 0; !res; i++) {
    try {
      res = await fetch(`http://localhost:${port}/api/health`)
    } catch {
      if (i >= 120) throw new Error('next dev did not start in 120 s')
      await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 1000))
    }
  }
  console.log(`${await res.text()} HTTP ${res.status}`)
} catch (e) {
  console.log(`FAIL ${e.message} - server log: ${logFile}`)
} finally {
  try {
    if (process.platform === 'win32') execSync(`taskkill /PID ${server.pid} /T /F`, { stdio: 'ignore' })
    else process.kill(-server.pid, 'SIGTERM')
  } catch {}
}
EOF
```

Expected: `{"ok":true,"region":"local","firestore":"ok"} HTTP 200`. The server is stopped when the script ends, so port 3103 is free again.

If you instead get `{"error":"internal"} HTTP 500` and the server log (`finflow-health-dev.log` in `%TEMP%`) shows `Failed to parse private key`, the env var does not hold the base64 of the original JSON file. Re-run exactly the command above. Do not paste the key by hand.

### Part B done: what later parts can use

| File | Exports |
|---|---|
| `lib/server/http-error.ts` | `class HttpError(status, message)`, `errorResponse(e): Response` |
| `lib/server/firebase-admin.ts` | `getAdmin(): { db: Firestore; auth: Auth }` (`server-only`) |
| `lib/server/auth.ts` | `isOrgMember(orgData, uid)`, `requireUserFromBearer(req) → { uid }` (401 `unauthorized`), `requireOrgMember(uid, orgId)` (403 `forbidden`) (`server-only`) |
| `app/api/health/route.ts` | `GET`, `runtime = 'nodejs'`, `dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` |
| `vitest.config.ts` | Unit config: `tests/unit/**/*.test.ts`, node env, `@` alias, `server-only` → `tests/unit/server-only-stub.ts`, `restoreMocks` + `unstubEnvs` |
| `package.json` | `test:unit` script; deps `firebase-admin@^14.5.0`, `server-only@^0.0.1`; devDeps `vitest@^5.0.5`, `@types/node@^24` |

Notes for later parts:
- **Mocking `getAdmin` in route tests.** Use `vi.mock('@/lib/server/firebase-admin', () => ({ getAdmin: mocks.getAdmin }))`, with `mocks` created by `vi.hoisted`. Vitest 5 **throws** if `vi.mock`/`vi.hoisted` is not at the top level of the file.
- **`vi.resetModules()` gives you new class identities.** After a reset, import `HttpError` from the fresh registry, not a static import, or `instanceof` fails.
- **Playwright and `tests/unit`.** While `playwright.config.ts` still has `testDir: './tests'`, Playwright's default `testMatch` also matches `tests/unit/**/*.test.ts` and would try to run the Vitest files. The e2e part's change to `testDir: './tests/e2e'` fixes this. Do not run `npx playwright test` between Part B and that change.

## Part C: Enable Banking hardening + remove test backdoor

This part removes the dev-only auth backdoor (`/api/test/auth`, `/test-login`) and the Playwright tests and fixtures built on it, then hardens all four `/api/enablebanking/*` routes. The changes are: a required Firebase ID token (401) plus org membership (403), an HMAC-SHA256-signed OAuth `state` with a 15-minute TTL (`lib/server/eb-state.ts`) that `callback` verifies together with membership before it exchanges the `code` (400/403), and an `ebFetch` helper so the settings UI sends the token. **Preconditions:** the platform part (Next 16, npm scripts `lint`/`typecheck`, `lib/enablebanking/jwt.ts` reading `process.env`) and the server-foundation part (`lib/server/http-error.ts`, `lib/server/firebase-admin.ts`, `lib/server/auth.ts`, the `firebase-admin` and `server-only` packages, and the Vitest setup: `vitest.config.ts`, `tests/unit/server-only-stub.ts`, `npm run test:unit`) must already be committed on the S00 working branch. Task C1 Step 1 checks this, and Part E later points Playwright `testDir` at `./tests/e2e` and rewrites `tests/e2e/landing.spec.ts`.

**Conventions for this part**

- Shell: Git Bash (POSIX sh) on the Windows host, cwd `F:/AI/finflow`. Every block starts with `cd /f/AI/finflow || exit 1`, and blocks that delete, write or commit also run the repo check from **How to read this plan**. Create and edit files with absolute paths (`F:/AI/finflow/...`).
- Line endings: the repo has `core.autocrlf=true`, so the working tree uses CRLF and the index uses LF. New files can be written with LF; Git normalises them on commit and may print `warning: LF will be replaced by CRLF`, which is expected. An exact-match edit tool has to match CRLF text. For the one edit of an existing file in this part (Task C5), use the provided script, which handles both line-ending styles.
- The unit tests in this part never touch the network or a real Firebase project. `getAdmin()` is replaced by an in-memory fake (`tests/unit/helpers/fake-admin.ts`), `createJwt()` by a stub that returns `eb-jwt`, and global `fetch` by `vi.fn()`. The **real** `requireUserFromBearer` / `requireOrgMember` from `lib/server/auth.ts` run against the fake data.
- Never print a secret value. `.env.local` is gitignored by the `.env*` rule in `.gitignore` (line 34).
- Tasks C3, C4 and C5 must land in the same PR. Between C3 and C5 the browser does not yet send a token, so connecting a bank only works again after C5.

### Task 10: Signed OAuth state — `lib/server/eb-state.ts` (TDD) (C2)

**Files:**
- Create: `lib/server/eb-state.ts`
- Test: `tests/unit/server/eb-state.test.ts`

Format, following the shared contract: `base64url(JSON.stringify({ o: orgId, u: uid, n: <16 hex chars>, e: <exp seconds> })) + "." + base64url(HMAC-SHA256(secret, payloadPart))`. The signature is compared with `crypto.timingSafeEqual` on equal-length buffers, and it is checked **before** the payload is parsed or the expiry is read. Errors: `HttpError(400, 'invalid state')` for any format or signature problem, `HttpError(400, 'expired state')` once `nowSec > e`, and `HttpError(500, 'server not configured')` for an empty secret.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `tests/unit/server/eb-state.test.ts`:

```ts
import { createHmac } from 'node:crypto'
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { EB_STATE_TTL_SECONDS, signEbState, verifyEbState } from '@/lib/server/eb-state'
import { HttpError } from '@/lib/server/http-error'

const SECRET = 'unit-test-secret-0123456789abcdef'
const NOW = 1_800_000_000
const P = { orgId: 'org-acme', uid: 'user-alice' }

function b64url(s: string): string {
  return Buffer.from(s, 'utf8').toString('base64url')
}

/** Builds a state string signed with `secret` around an arbitrary payload (used to forge edge cases). */
function forge(payloadJson: string, secret: string = SECRET): string {
  const payloadPart = b64url(payloadJson)
  const sig = createHmac('sha256', secret).update(payloadPart).digest('base64url')
  return `${payloadPart}.${sig}`
}

function expectHttpError(fn: () => unknown, status: number, message: string): void {
  let caught: unknown
  try {
    fn()
  } catch (e) {
    caught = e
  }
  expect(caught).toBeInstanceOf(HttpError)
  expect((caught as HttpError).status).toBe(status)
  expect((caught as HttpError).message).toBe(message)
}

describe('eb-state', () => {
  it('uses a 15 minute TTL', () => {
    expect(EB_STATE_TTL_SECONDS).toBe(900)
  })

  it('round-trips orgId and uid', () => {
    const state = signEbState(P, SECRET, NOW)
    expect(verifyEbState(state, SECRET, NOW)).toEqual(P)
  })

  it('produces base64url(JSON {o,u,n,e}) + "." + base64url(HMAC-SHA256)', () => {
    const state = signEbState(P, SECRET, NOW)
    const [payloadPart, sigPart, extra] = state.split('.')
    expect(extra).toBeUndefined()
    expect(payloadPart).toMatch(/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$/)
    expect(sigPart).toBe(createHmac('sha256', SECRET).update(payloadPart).digest('base64url'))
    const payload = JSON.parse(Buffer.from(payloadPart, 'base64url').toString('utf8'))
    expect(Object.keys(payload)).toEqual(['o', 'u', 'n', 'e'])
    expect(payload.o).toBe('org-acme')
    expect(payload.u).toBe('user-alice')
    expect(payload.n).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{16}$/)
    expect(payload.e).toBe(NOW + 900)
  })

  it('uses a fresh nonce on every call', () => {
    expect(signEbState(P, SECRET, NOW)).not.toBe(signEbState(P, SECRET, NOW))
  })

  it('defaults nowSec to the current time', () => {
    const state = signEbState(P, SECRET)
    expect(verifyEbState(state, SECRET)).toEqual(P)
  })

  it('is still valid exactly at the expiry second', () => {
    const state = signEbState(P, SECRET, NOW)
    expect(verifyEbState(state, SECRET, NOW + EB_STATE_TTL_SECONDS)).toEqual(P)
  })

  it('rejects an expired state with 400 "expired state"', () => {
    const state = signEbState(P, SECRET, NOW)
    expectHttpError(() => verifyEbState(state, SECRET, NOW + EB_STATE_TTL_SECONDS + 1), 400, 'expired state')
  })

  it('rejects a tampered payload', () => {
    const state = signEbState(P, SECRET, NOW)
    const [payloadPart, sigPart] = state.split('.')
    const payload = JSON.parse(Buffer.from(payloadPart, 'base64url').toString('utf8'))
    payload.o = 'org-evil'
    const tampered = `${b64url(JSON.stringify(payload))}.${sigPart}`
    expectHttpError(() => verifyEbState(tampered, SECRET, NOW), 400, 'invalid state')
  })

  it('rejects a tampered signature', () => {
    const state = signEbState(P, SECRET, NOW)
    const [payloadPart, sigPart] = state.split('.')
    const flipped = (sigPart[0] === 'A' ? 'B' : 'A') + sigPart.slice(1)
    expectHttpError(() => verifyEbState(`${payloadPart}.${flipped}`, SECRET, NOW), 400, 'invalid state')
  })

  it('rejects a signature truncated by one character', () => {
    const state = signEbState(P, SECRET, NOW)
    expectHttpError(() => verifyEbState(state.slice(0, -1), SECRET, NOW), 400, 'invalid state')
  })

  it('rejects a state signed with a different secret', () => {
    const state = signEbState(P, 'some-other-secret', NOW)
    expectHttpError(() => verifyEbState(state, SECRET, NOW), 400, 'invalid state')
  })

  it('reports a forged expired state as invalid, not expired (signature is checked first)', () => {
    const forged = forge(JSON.stringify({ o: 'org-acme', u: 'user-alice', n: '0011223344556677', e: 1 }), 'attacker-secret')
    expectHttpError(() => verifyEbState(forged, SECRET, NOW), 400, 'invalid state')
  })

  it.each([
    ['empty string', ''],
    ['no dot', 'abc'],
    ['three parts', 'a.b.c'],
    ['only a dot', '.'],
    ['empty signature', 'abc.'],
    ['empty payload', '.abc'],
    ['non-base64url payload', 'a$b.c'],
    ['non-base64url signature', 'abc.d+f/'],
    ['whitespace', ' . '],
  ])('rejects a malformed state (%s)', (_label, state) => {
    expectHttpError(() => verifyEbState(state, SECRET, NOW), 400, 'invalid state')
  })

  it.each([
    ['not JSON', 'not json'],
    ['JSON null', 'null'],
    ['JSON array', '[]'],
    ['empty orgId', JSON.stringify({ o: '', u: 'user-alice', n: '0011223344556677', e: NOW + 60 })],
    ['missing uid', JSON.stringify({ o: 'org-acme', n: '0011223344556677', e: NOW + 60 })],
    ['missing nonce', JSON.stringify({ o: 'org-acme', u: 'user-alice', e: NOW + 60 })],
    ['string expiry', JSON.stringify({ o: 'org-acme', u: 'user-alice', n: '0011223344556677', e: String(NOW + 60) })],
    ['fractional expiry', JSON.stringify({ o: 'org-acme', u: 'user-alice', n: '0011223344556677', e: NOW + 0.5 })],
  ])('rejects a correctly signed but malformed payload (%s)', (_label, payloadJson) => {
    expectHttpError(() => verifyEbState(forge(payloadJson), SECRET, NOW), 400, 'invalid state')
  })

  it('throws 500 "server not configured" when the secret is empty', () => {
    expectHttpError(() => signEbState(P, '', NOW), 500, 'server not configured')
    const state = signEbState(P, SECRET, NOW)
    expectHttpError(() => verifyEbState(state, '', NOW), 500, 'server not configured')
  })

  it('refuses to sign without orgId or uid', () => {
    expect(() => signEbState({ orgId: '', uid: 'user-alice' }, SECRET, NOW)).toThrow('signEbState: orgId and uid are required')
    expect(() => signEbState({ orgId: 'org-acme', uid: '' }, SECRET, NOW)).toThrow('signEbState: orgId and uid are required')
  })
})
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `lib/server/eb-state.ts`**

```ts
import 'server-only'
import { createHmac, randomBytes, timingSafeEqual } from 'node:crypto'
import { HttpError } from '@/lib/server/http-error'

/** Lifetime of an Enable Banking OAuth `state` value: 15 minutes. */
export const EB_STATE_TTL_SECONDS = 900

/** Wire payload. Short keys keep the state (sent through the bank redirect) compact. */
type EbStatePayload = { o: string; u: string; n: string; e: number }

const BASE64URL_RE = /^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$/
const MAX_STATE_LENGTH = 2048

function currentSec(): number {
  return Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000)
}

function assertSecret(secret: string): void {
  if (typeof secret !== 'string' || secret.length === 0) {
    throw new HttpError(500, 'server not configured')
  }
}

function hmac(payloadPart: string, secret: string): string {
  return createHmac('sha256', secret).update(payloadPart).digest('base64url')
}

function isPayload(value: unknown): value is EbStatePayload {
  if (typeof value !== 'object' || value === null) return false
  const r = value as Record<string, unknown>
  return (
    typeof r.o === 'string' &&
    r.o.length > 0 &&
    typeof r.u === 'string' &&
    r.u.length > 0 &&
    typeof r.n === 'string' &&
    r.n.length > 0 &&
    typeof r.e === 'number' &&
    Number.isInteger(r.e)
  )
}

/**
 * Signs `{ orgId, uid }` into an opaque `state` for POST https://api.enablebanking.com/auth.
 * Format: base64url(JSON {o, u, n, e}) + "." + base64url(HMAC-SHA256(secret, payloadPart)).
 */
export function signEbState(p: { orgId: string; uid: string }, secret: string, nowSec: number = currentSec()): string {
  assertSecret(secret)
  if (!p.orgId || !p.uid) throw new Error('signEbState: orgId and uid are required')
  const payload: EbStatePayload = {
    o: p.orgId,
    u: p.uid,
    n: randomBytes(8).toString('hex'),
    e: nowSec + EB_STATE_TTL_SECONDS,
  }
  const payloadPart = Buffer.from(JSON.stringify(payload), 'utf8').toString('base64url')
  return `${payloadPart}.${hmac(payloadPart, secret)}`
}

/**
 * Verifies a `state` produced by signEbState.
 * Throws HttpError(400, 'invalid state') for any format or signature problem and
 * HttpError(400, 'expired state') when the signature is valid but the TTL has passed.
 */
export function verifyEbState(state: string, secret: string, nowSec: number = currentSec()): { orgId: string; uid: string } {
  assertSecret(secret)
  const invalid = () => new HttpError(400, 'invalid state')

  if (typeof state !== 'string' || state.length === 0 || state.length > MAX_STATE_LENGTH) throw invalid()
  const parts = state.split('.')
  if (parts.length !== 2) throw invalid()
  const [payloadPart, sigPart] = parts
  if (!BASE64URL_RE.test(payloadPart) || !BASE64URL_RE.test(sigPart)) throw invalid()

  // Constant-time comparison of the canonical base64url signature strings.
  const expected = Buffer.from(hmac(payloadPart, secret), 'utf8')
  const given = Buffer.from(sigPart, 'utf8')
  if (given.length !== expected.length || !timingSafeEqual(given, expected)) throw invalid()

  let payload: unknown
  try {
    payload = JSON.parse(Buffer.from(payloadPart, 'base64url').toString('utf8'))
  } catch {
    throw invalid()
  }
  if (!isPayload(payload)) throw invalid()
  if (nowSec > payload.e) throw new HttpError(400, 'expired state')
  return { orgId: payload.o, uid: payload.u }
}
```

- Test: `tests/unit/api/enablebanking/session.test.ts`

- Modify (full replacement): `app/api/enablebanking/accounts/route.ts` (currently lines 1–22)
- Modify (full replacement): `app/api/enablebanking/institutions/route.ts` (currently lines 1–27)

Behaviour after this task:

| Route | Auth | Membership | Success payload (unchanged) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `POST /api/enablebanking/session` `{ aspspName, aspspCountry?, orgId }` | 401 without a valid `Authorization: Bearer <ID token>` | 403 unless `requireOrgMember(uid, orgId)` passes | `{ url, authorizationId }` |
| `GET /api/enablebanking/accounts?sessionId&orgId` | 401 | 403 | `{ accounts }` |
| `GET /api/enablebanking/institutions?country` | 401 | none (no org data) | `{ institutions: [{ id, name, country, logo, bic }] }` |

Further changes:
- Inputs are validated (400). `orgId` and `sessionId` must match `/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}$/` and `country`/`aspspCountry` must match `/^[A-Z]{2}$/`, so neither can inject a path or query into the upstream URL.
- The request field `country` becomes `aspspCountry`, as the contract specifies. The client is switched in Task C5.
- Upstream Enable Banking failures return `502 { error: 'enable banking error' }` and the upstream body is logged on the server only. Previously the routes forwarded the upstream status and body to the browser.
- The upstream endpoints do **not** change. `accounts` keeps calling `GET https://api.enablebanking.com/sessions/{session_id}/accounts` and keeps its success mapping `{ accounts: data.accounts ?? data ?? [] }`. The Enable Banking API reference (https://enablebanking.com/docs/api/reference/, "Get session data") has no such path, only `GET /sessions/{session_id}`, whose `accounts` is the list of account ids. No UI calls this route, and S00 adds no product behaviour, so the endpoint fix is left to S09 (Enable Banking import) and recorded as known debt in `PROGRESS.md` (Task 24 Step 6).
- All three routes `export const runtime = 'nodejs'`. Helpers stay unexported because Next.js rejects unknown exports from `route.ts`.


- [ ] **Step 2: Create the shared fake for `getAdmin()`**

Create `tests/unit/helpers/fake-admin.ts`:

```ts
import { vi } from 'vitest'

/**
 * In-memory stand-in for getAdmin() from '@/lib/server/firebase-admin'.
 * Route tests mock that module with `{ getAdmin: () => fakeAdmin }`, so the REAL
 * requireUserFromBearer / requireOrgMember from '@/lib/server/auth' run against this data.
 * Only `organizations/{orgId}` documents are modelled; both db.doc(path) and
 * db.collection(name).doc(id) access styles are supported.
 */
export const fakeOrgs = new Map<string, Record<string, unknown>>()

/** Firebase ID token -> uid accepted by the fake verifyIdToken. */
export const fakeTokens = new Map<string, string>()

export const TEST_EB_STATE_SECRET = 'route-test-secret-0123456789abcdef'
export const TEST_APP_URL = 'https://finflow.test'

function snapshotFor(path: string) {
  const segments = path.split('/')
  const data =
    segments.length === 2 && segments[0] === 'organizations' ? fakeOrgs.get(segments[1]) : undefined
  return {
    id: segments[segments.length - 1],
    exists: data !== undefined,
    data: () => data,
    get: (field: string) => (data === undefined ? undefined : data[field]),
  }
}

function docRef(path: string) {
  return {
    id: path.split('/').pop(),
    path,
    get: async () => snapshotFor(path),
  }
}

export const fakeAdmin = {
  db: {
    doc: (path: string) => docRef(path),
    collection: (name: string) => ({ doc: (id: string) => docRef(`${name}/${id}`) }),
  },
  auth: {
    verifyIdToken: vi.fn(async (token: string) => {
      const uid = fakeTokens.get(token)
      if (!uid) throw new Error('auth/invalid-id-token')
      return { uid }
    }),
  },
}

/** Resets the fixture: alice owns org-acme; mallory is signed in but not a member of anything. */
export function resetFakeAdmin(): void {
  fakeOrgs.clear()
  fakeTokens.clear()
  fakeTokens.set('token-alice', 'user-alice')
  fakeTokens.set('token-mallory', 'user-mallory')
  fakeOrgs.set('org-acme', {
    name: 'Acme',
    currency: 'PLN',
    ownerId: 'user-alice',
    plan: 'trial',
    members: { 'user-alice': { role: 'owner' } },
  })
  fakeAdmin.auth.verifyIdToken.mockClear()
}

/** A JSON Response, as returned by the mocked global fetch for Enable Banking calls. */
export function upstreamJson(body: unknown, status = 200): Response {
  return new Response(JSON.stringify(body), {
    status,
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
  })
}
```

```ts
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest'
import {
  TEST_APP_URL,
  TEST_EB_STATE_SECRET,
  fakeAdmin,
  resetFakeAdmin,
  upstreamJson,
} from '@/tests/unit/helpers/fake-admin'
import { verifyEbState } from '@/lib/server/eb-state'
import { POST } from '@/app/api/enablebanking/session/route'

vi.mock('@/lib/server/firebase-admin', async () => {
  const { fakeAdmin: admin } = await import('@/tests/unit/helpers/fake-admin')
  return { getAdmin: () => admin }
})
vi.mock('@/lib/enablebanking/jwt', () => ({ createJwt: vi.fn(async () => 'eb-jwt') }))

const fetchMock = vi.fn()

function sessionRequest(body: unknown, token?: string): Request {
  const headers: Record<string, string> = { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }
  if (token) headers.Authorization = `Bearer ${token}`
  return new Request('http://localhost:3000/api/enablebanking/session', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers,
    body: JSON.stringify(body),
  })
}

const VALID_BODY = { aspspName: 'Mock ASPSP', aspspCountry: 'FI', orgId: 'org-acme' }

describe('POST /api/enablebanking/session', () => {
  let errorSpy: { mockRestore: () => void }

  beforeEach(() => {
    resetFakeAdmin()
    fetchMock.mockReset()
    vi.stubGlobal('fetch', fetchMock)
    vi.stubEnv('EB_STATE_SECRET', TEST_EB_STATE_SECRET)
    vi.stubEnv('NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL', TEST_APP_URL)
    errorSpy = vi.spyOn(console, 'error').mockImplementation(() => {})
  })

  afterEach(() => {
    errorSpy.mockRestore()
    vi.unstubAllGlobals()
    vi.unstubAllEnvs()
  })

  it('returns 401 without an Authorization header and never calls Enable Banking', async () => {
    const res = await POST(sessionRequest(VALID_BODY))
    expect(res.status).toBe(401)
    expect(typeof (await res.json()).error).toBe('string')
    expect(fetchMock).not.toHaveBeenCalled()
  })

  it('returns 401 for an invalid ID token', async () => {
    const res = await POST(sessionRequest(VALID_BODY, 'token-forged'))
    expect(res.status).toBe(401)
    expect(fetchMock).not.toHaveBeenCalled()
  })

  it('returns 400 when orgId is missing', async () => {
    const res = await POST(sessionRequest({ aspspName: 'Mock ASPSP' }, 'token-alice'))
    expect(res.status).toBe(400)
    expect(await res.json()).toEqual({ error: 'Missing params' })
  })

  it('returns 400 when aspspName is missing', async () => {
    const res = await POST(sessionRequest({ orgId: 'org-acme' }, 'token-alice'))
    expect(res.status).toBe(400)
    expect(await res.json()).toEqual({ error: 'Missing params' })
  })

  it('returns 400 for a malformed aspspCountry', async () => {
    const res = await POST(sessionRequest({ ...VALID_BODY, aspspCountry: 'pl;' }, 'token-alice'))
    expect(res.status).toBe(400)
    expect(await res.json()).toEqual({ error: 'invalid country' })
  })

  it('returns 403 when the caller is not a member of the org', async () => {
    const res = await POST(sessionRequest(VALID_BODY, 'token-mallory'))
    expect(res.status).toBe(403)
    expect(fetchMock).not.toHaveBeenCalled()
  })

  it('returns 403 when the org does not exist', async () => {
    const res = await POST(sessionRequest({ ...VALID_BODY, orgId: 'org-ghost' }, 'token-alice'))
    expect(res.status).toBe(403)
    expect(fetchMock).not.toHaveBeenCalled()
  })

  it('starts the authorization with a signed state and returns { url, authorizationId }', async () => {
    fetchMock.mockResolvedValueOnce(
      upstreamJson({
        url: 'https://auth.enablebanking.com/ais/start?sessionid=73100c65',
        authorization_id: '73100c65-c54d-46a1-87d1-aa3effde435a',
        psu_id_hash: 'abc',
      }),
    )

    const res = await POST(sessionRequest(VALID_BODY, 'token-alice'))

    expect(res.status).toBe(200)
    expect(await res.json()).toEqual({
      url: 'https://auth.enablebanking.com/ais/start?sessionid=73100c65',
      authorizationId: '73100c65-c54d-46a1-87d1-aa3effde435a',
    })
    expect(fakeAdmin.auth.verifyIdToken).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1)
    expect(fetchMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1)
    const [url, init] = fetchMock.mock.calls[0] as [string, RequestInit]
    expect(url).toBe('https://api.enablebanking.com/auth')
    expect(init.method).toBe('POST')
    expect((init.headers as Record<string, string>).Authorization).toBe('Bearer eb-jwt')
    const sent = JSON.parse(String(init.body))
    expect(sent.aspsp).toEqual({ name: 'Mock ASPSP', country: 'FI' })
    expect(sent.redirect_url).toBe(`${TEST_APP_URL}/api/enablebanking/callback`)
    expect(sent.psu_type).toBe('personal')
    expect(sent.access.balances).toBe(true)
    expect(sent.access.transactions).toBe(true)
    expect(verifyEbState(sent.state, TEST_EB_STATE_SECRET)).toEqual({ orgId: 'org-acme', uid: 'user-alice' })
  })

  it('defaults aspspCountry to PL', async () => {
    fetchMock.mockResolvedValueOnce(upstreamJson({ url: 'https://x.test', authorization_id: 'a1' }))
    await POST(sessionRequest({ aspspName: 'Bank Millennium', orgId: 'org-acme' }, 'token-alice'))
    const [, init] = fetchMock.mock.calls[0] as [string, RequestInit]
    expect(JSON.parse(String(init.body)).aspsp).toEqual({ name: 'Bank Millennium', country: 'PL' })
  })

  it('maps an Enable Banking failure to 502 without leaking the upstream body', async () => {
    fetchMock.mockResolvedValueOnce(upstreamJson({ message: 'redirect_url not allowed' }, 422))
    const res = await POST(sessionRequest(VALID_BODY, 'token-alice'))
    expect(res.status).toBe(502)
    expect(await res.json()).toEqual({ error: 'enable banking error' })
  })

  it('returns 500 "server not configured" when EB_STATE_SECRET is empty', async () => {
    vi.stubEnv('EB_STATE_SECRET', '')
    const res = await POST(sessionRequest(VALID_BODY, 'token-alice'))
    expect(res.status).toBe(500)
    expect(await res.json()).toEqual({ error: 'server not configured' })
    expect(fetchMock).not.toHaveBeenCalled()
  })
})
```

```ts
import { createJwt } from '@/lib/enablebanking/jwt'
import { requireOrgMember, requireUserFromBearer } from '@/lib/server/auth'
import { signEbState } from '@/lib/server/eb-state'
import { errorResponse, HttpError } from '@/lib/server/http-error'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'

const BASE = 'https://api.enablebanking.com'
const ID_RE = /^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}$/
const COUNTRY_RE = /^[A-Z]{2}$/

// POST /api/enablebanking/session  { aspspName, aspspCountry?, orgId }
// Requires `Authorization: Bearer <Firebase ID token>` of a member of `orgId`.
export async function POST(req: Request) {
  try {
    const { uid } = await requireUserFromBearer(req)

    const body: unknown = await req.json().catch(() => null)
    const { aspspName, aspspCountry = 'PL', orgId } = (body ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>
    if (typeof aspspName !== 'string' || aspspName.length === 0 || aspspName.length > 200) {
      throw new HttpError(400, 'Missing params')
    }
    if (typeof orgId !== 'string' || !ID_RE.test(orgId)) throw new HttpError(400, 'Missing params')
    if (typeof aspspCountry !== 'string' || !COUNTRY_RE.test(aspspCountry)) {
      throw new HttpError(400, 'invalid country')
    }

    await requireOrgMember(uid, orgId)

    const state = signEbState({ orgId, uid }, process.env.EB_STATE_SECRET!)
    const token = await createJwt()
    const appUrl = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL || new URL(req.url).origin

    const res = await fetch(`${BASE}/auth`, {
      method: 'POST',
      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}`, 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
      body: JSON.stringify({
        access: {
          valid_until: new Date(Date.now() + 90 * 86400_000).toISOString(),
          balances: true,
          transactions: true,
        },
        aspsp: { name: aspspName, country: aspspCountry },
        redirect_url: `${appUrl}/api/enablebanking/callback`,
        psu_type: 'personal',
        state,
      }),
    })

    if (!res.ok) {
      console.error('Enable Banking POST /auth failed:', res.status, await res.text())
      throw new HttpError(502, 'enable banking error')
    }
    const data = await res.json()
    return Response.json({ url: data.url, authorizationId: data.authorization_id })
  } catch (e) {
    return errorResponse(e)
  }
}
```

```ts
import { createJwt } from '@/lib/enablebanking/jwt'
import { requireOrgMember, requireUserFromBearer } from '@/lib/server/auth'
import { errorResponse, HttpError } from '@/lib/server/http-error'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'

const BASE = 'https://api.enablebanking.com'
const ID_RE = /^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}$/

// GET /api/enablebanking/accounts?sessionId=xxx&orgId=yyy
// Requires `Authorization: Bearer <Firebase ID token>` of a member of `orgId`.
export async function GET(req: Request) {
  try {
    const { uid } = await requireUserFromBearer(req)

    const params = new URL(req.url).searchParams
    const sessionId = params.get('sessionId')
    const orgId = params.get('orgId')
    if (!sessionId || !ID_RE.test(sessionId)) throw new HttpError(400, 'Missing sessionId')
    if (!orgId || !ID_RE.test(orgId)) throw new HttpError(400, 'Missing orgId')

    await requireOrgMember(uid, orgId)

    const token = await createJwt()
    // Upstream call unchanged in S00 (no UI uses this route). Known debt for S09: the
    // Enable Banking API has no /sessions/{id}/accounts; GET /sessions/{id} returns `accounts`.
    const res = await fetch(`${BASE}/sessions/${sessionId}/accounts`, {
      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` },
    })
    if (!res.ok) {
      console.error('Enable Banking GET /sessions/{id}/accounts failed:', res.status, await res.text())
      throw new HttpError(502, 'enable banking error')
    }
    const data = await res.json()
    return Response.json({ accounts: data.accounts ?? data ?? [] })
  } catch (e) {
    return errorResponse(e)
  }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 13: Replace `app/api/enablebanking/institutions/route.ts` (whole file)**

```ts
import { createJwt } from '@/lib/enablebanking/jwt'
import { requireUserFromBearer } from '@/lib/server/auth'
import { errorResponse, HttpError } from '@/lib/server/http-error'

export const runtime = 'nodejs'

const BASE = 'https://api.enablebanking.com'
const COUNTRY_RE = /^[A-Z]{2}$/

type Aspsp = { name: string; country: string; logo?: string; bic?: string }

// GET /api/enablebanking/institutions?country=PL
// Requires `Authorization: Bearer <Firebase ID token>` (any signed-in user; no org data involved).
export async function GET(req: Request) {
  try {
    await requireUserFromBearer(req)

    const country = new URL(req.url).searchParams.get('country') ?? 'PL'
    if (!COUNTRY_RE.test(country)) throw new HttpError(400, 'invalid country')

    const token = await createJwt()
    const res = await fetch(`${BASE}/aspsps?country=${country}`, {
      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` },
    })
    if (!res.ok) {
      console.error('Enable Banking GET /aspsps failed:', res.status, await res.text())
      throw new HttpError(502, 'enable banking error')
    }
    const data = await res.json()
    const institutions = ((data.aspsps ?? []) as Aspsp[]).map((b) => ({
      id: b.name,
      name: b.name,
      country: b.country,
      logo: b.logo ?? null,
      bic: b.bic ?? null,
    }))
    return Response.json({ institutions })
  } catch (e) {
    return errorResponse(e)
  }
}
```
